Appendix C
Metatheory

C.1.
Data-Race Freedom

Simplifying Assumption.

For simplicity, we ignore
RMW
accesses in this subsection.
An idea to overcome this limitation is
to replace our formulation of
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistency
with one that (like the original formulation of
RC11

(
Lahav et al., 2017
)
)
models a
RMW
access as either a read
or a pair of a write and a read related by a
{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\sf{rmw}}
relation.

Definition C.1 (
SC
-Consistency).

An execution graph
(G,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}})
is
SC
-consistent

if the following condition holds:

•

\mathit{acyclic}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}
(
SC-Coherence
)

Definition C.2 (Racy).

Let
R
be a relation on events.
An execution graph
G
is
R
-racy
if
it contains a pair of events
\left(a,\,b\right)

that forms a
R
-race and for which
either
a
or
b
is not
sc
:

G\;\textit{is $R$-racy }\;\iff\;\exists\,a,\,b.\;\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\texttt{race}}(R)\land(a.\texttt{md}\neq{\texttt{sc}}\lor b.\texttt{md}\neq{\texttt{sc}})

Definition C.3 (Data-Race Free).

Let
R
be a relation on events.
A program
p
is

data-race free with respect to
R
,
or simply
is
R
-race free
,
if every
SC
-consistent execution graph
G

associated with
p
is not
R
-racy:

p\;\textit{is $R$-race free }\;\iff\;\begin{array}[]{@{\,}l@{}}\forall\,G,\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,a,\,b.\\
\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{@{\,}l@{\,}}\mathit{toPool}(p)\;/\;\mathit{Init}\;\longrightarrow^{*}\;\_\;/\;G\\
(G,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}})\;\textit{is {SC}-consistent}\\
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\texttt{race}}(R)\end{array}\right)\implies a.\texttt{md}=b.\texttt{md}={\texttt{sc}}\end{array}

We show that our extended model
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
enjoys the data-race-freedom property;
that is,
the semantics assigned by
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
to a

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-race free program coincides with
SC
:

Theorem C.4 (
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
- Data-Race Freedom).

\forall\,p.\;p\;\text{is ${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}$-race free }\implies\llbracket\hskip 2.0pt{p}\hskip 1.0pt\rrbracket_{\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}}=\llbracket\hskip 2.0pt{p}\hskip 1.0pt\rrbracket_{\textsf{SC}}

Proof.

From Corollary
C.10
,
it follows that
p
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race free.
Let
G
be a
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent graph associated with
p
.
By Lemma
C.12
,
the graph is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.
Then, by Lemma
C.11
,
we conclude that
G
is
SC
-consistent.
∎

We now state and prove the lemmas on which the proof
of Theorem
C.4
relies.

Remark

A
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent (or
RC11
-consistent)
that is not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-racy is not necessarily
SC
-consistent:

Notation.

The relation
R|_{A}
denotes the restriction of
R

to a set
A
:
R|_{A}\;\triangleq\;[A];\,{R};\,[A]
.
We use the abbreviation
R|_{{\texttt{sc}}}
for the restriction of
R

to
{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}
:
R|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\;\triangleq\;R|_{{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}}
.
The graph
G|_{A}

is the restriction of
G
to
A
:
G|_{A}\;\triangleq\;G.\{{\texttt{E}}:=(G.{\texttt{E}}\cap{A})\}
.

Definition C.5 (
R
-closed).

A set
A
is
closed with respect to a relation
R
,
or simply
R
-closed
,
if the inclusion
R;\,[A]\subseteq[A];\,{R}

holds.

Lemma C.6.

Let
(G,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}})
be a

\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent (resp.
SC
-consistent)
graph associated with a program
p
,
and
let
D\subseteq G.{\texttt{E}}
be a
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed set.
The graph
(G|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})
is

\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent (resp.
SC
-consistent).
Moreover, the graph
G|_{D}
is associated with
p
,
and
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}|_{D}

(the restriction of
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
to
D
)
is included in
G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}

(the happens-before relation derived
from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}
).

Proof.

Because
D
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed,
the following inclusion holds:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0}\sf{sw}}|_{D}&\subseteq&[D];\,[{\texttt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rel}}}];\,{([{\texttt{F}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}})}^{?};\,[{\texttt{W}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rlx}}}];\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}^{+};\,[{\texttt{R}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rlx}}}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{[{\texttt{F}}]})}^{?};\,[{\texttt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{acq}}}];\,[D]\\
&\subseteq&[{\texttt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rel}}}];\,{([{\texttt{F}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D})}^{?};\,[{\texttt{W}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rlx}}}];\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}}^{+};\,[{\texttt{R}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{rlx}}}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D};\,{[{\texttt{F}}]})}^{?};\,[{\texttt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{acq}}}]\\
&\subseteq&G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0}\sf{sw}}\end{array}

It is then easy to see that
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}|_{D}\subseteq G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
.
∎

The restriction of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
to
D
in the statement of
Lemma
C.6
is not necessary.
However, these restrictions result in a stronger statement,
because
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistency (resp.
SC
-consistency)
is monotonic with respect to both
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
.

Lemma C.7.

The set
\mathit{dom}({R}^{*};\,[A])
is
R
-closed,
for any set
A
and relation
R
.

Proof.

Let
D
stand for
\mathit{dom}({R}^{*};\,[A])
,
and let
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{R};\,[D]
.
It suffices to show that
a\in{D}
.
There exists
c
such that and
\left(b,\,c\right)\in{R}^{*};\,[A]
.
Therefore
\left(a,\,c\right)\in{R};\,{R}^{*};\,[A]\subseteq{R}^{*};\,[A]
,
which implies that
a\in{D}
.
∎

Corollary C.8.

Let
G
be a graph,
and
D
be a subset of
G.{\texttt{E}}
.
The set
\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[D])

is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed.

Lemma C.9.

Let
p
be a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-race free program
and let
(G,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}})

be a
SC
-consistent graph associated with
p
.
The relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
is included in
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
.

Proof.

We introduce the following notation:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}R&\triangleq&{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\\
S&\triangleq&{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\setminus{R}\end{array}

Suppose by contradiction that
S
is non-empty.
Let
\left(a,\,b\right)
be a pair in
S

for which
b
is minimal with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.
(Such a pair exists because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
is acyclic in
G
.)

Claim 1

The pair
\left(a,\,b\right)
belongs to
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}
.

Proof.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}
,
then
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\subseteq{R}
.
∎

Let
D

be the set
\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])
.

Claim 2

If
c
belongs to
D
, then
c\in\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])
.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that there exists
c\neq a,b

such that
c\in\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])

but
c\notin\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])
.
Then it must be the case that

c\in\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,{S};\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}]).

However, this implies the existence of a pair in
S\setminus\{\left(a,\,b\right)\}

that contradicts the minimality of
b
with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.
∎

Claim 3

The graph
(G|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})

is not
(G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}})
-racy,
SC
-consistent,
and associated with
p
.

Proof.

That the graph is
SC
-consistent and associated with
p

is a direct consequence from Lemma
C.6
.
Let
c,d\in{D}
be a pair of distinct events
acting on the same location.
Because
G
is not
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-racy,
it follows that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}\,\cup\,G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}^{-1}
.
It follows from Lemma
C.6

that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}\,\cup\,G|_{D}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}^{-1}
.
∎

Claim 4

The events
a
and
b
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that there exists
c\in{D}

such that
\left(a,\,c\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
.
Because
c\in{D}
,
either
\left(c,\,a\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}

or
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
.
If
\left(c,\,a\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
then
\left(a,\,c\right)
violates
the acyclicity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.
If
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
then, from
Claim 2
,
it follows that

\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{*}
.
But then
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{*}\subseteq{R}
,
a contradiction to
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{S}
.

Suppose by contradiction that there exists
c\in{D}

such that
\left(b,\,c\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
.
If
\left(c,\,a\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
then
(a,\,b,\,c)
violates
the acyclicity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.
Analogously,
if
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
then
(b,\,c)
violates
the acyclicity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.
∎

Let
e\in{D}
be the write event
that immediately precedes
a
in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
,
that is,
\left(e,\,a\right)\in{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{\textnormal{{imm}}}
.
We define the graph
G^{\prime}
, and the relations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}

as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}G^{\prime}&\;\triangleq&G|_{D}.\{{\texttt{lab}}:=G|_{D}.{\texttt{lab}}[b\mapsto{\texttt{R}}^{\mathit{md}_{2}}(x,\,v)]\}\;\;\text{where}\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}G|_{D}.{\texttt{lab}}(e)={\texttt{W}}^{\mathit{md}_{1}}(x,\,v)\\
G|_{D}.{\texttt{lab}}(b)={\texttt{R}}^{\mathit{md}_{2}}(x,\,\_)\end{array}\right.\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime}&\;\triangleq&({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}\setminus\{\left(a,\,b\right)\})\cup\{\left(e,\,b\right)\}\end{array}

Claim 5

The graph
(G^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})

is
SC
-consistent, and associated with
p
.

Proof.

Suppose, by contradiction, that there is a cycle
C

in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}
denote the derived reads-before relation
G^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}
.
Because
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}|_{D}=\{\left(b,\,a\right)\}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}=\{\left(e,\,b\right)\}
,
the only edges
that could possibly be in
C

but not in
G|_{D}
are
\left(b,\,a\right)

and
\left(e,\,b\right)
.
If
C
includes the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime}
edge
\left(e,\,b\right)
,
then it must be followed by a
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}
edge,
because
b
is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal read event.
By definition of the reads-before relation,
it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime};\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}
is included
in
G^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
,
which coincides with
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}
.
The sequence
[e];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime};\,[b];\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}

can thus be exchanged with
[e];\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}
.
Moreover,
if
C
includes the
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime}
edge
\left(b,\,a\right)
,
then it must be followed by a
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}

edge because

a
is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal write event.
The sequence
[b];\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}^{\prime};\,[a];\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}

can thus be exchanged with
[b];\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}|_{D}
.
Performing all these exchanges yields a cycle in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}|_{D}
,
a contradiction to the
SC
-consistency of
G|_{D}
.

∎

Claim 6

The graph
(G^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})

is
(G^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}})
-racy.

Proof.

The events
a
and
b
are distinct,
they act on the same location,
at least one of them is a write event.
Moreover,

\mathit{min}\,(a.\texttt{md},b.\texttt{md})\neq{\texttt{sc}}
,
because otherwise
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{R}
.
Finally,
because both are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal,
and because
a\notin\mathit{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime})
,
it follows that
[\{a,b\}];\,G^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}\subseteq[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime})}^{+}=\emptyset
.
Therefore,
the pair
\left(a,\,b\right)
forms a
(G^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}})
-race.
∎

From

Claims 5

and

6
,
it follows that
the graph
(G^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{\prime},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})

contradicts the assumption that
p

is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-race free.
∎

Corollary C.10.

If a program
p
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
-race free,
then it is
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-race free.

Lemma C.11.

Let
G
be a
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent graph.
If
G
is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy,
then
G
is
SC
-consistent.

Proof.

Because
G
is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy,
the following inclusions hold:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\;}c@{\;}r@{\;}c@{\;\;}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}&\subseteq&{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}&\,\cup&{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}&\subseteq&{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}&\,\cup&{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}&\subseteq&{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}&\,\cup&{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\end{array}

We can use these inclusions to show that
the violation of
SC
-consistency yields
the following chain of implications:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})\\
\begin{array}[]{@{\implies}l@{}}\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+})\\
\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})\end{array}\end{array}

The internal edges of a cycle in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}

are separated by external edges
whose domain and codomain is included in
{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}
.
Consequently,
the domain and codomain of
the internal edges
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}

must also be included in
{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}
.
It follows that
the relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e}|_{{\texttt{sc}}}

is cyclic,
a contradiction to Condition
SC
.
∎

Lemma C.12.

If a program
p
is
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-race free,
then
every
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent graph associated with
p

is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.

Proof.

Let
(G,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}})
be a
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent
graph associated with
p
.
Suppose by contradiction that
G
is

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.
In other words, suppose that the set
S\triangleq{\texttt{race}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})\setminus{({\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}\times{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}})}

is non-empty.
Let
\left(a,\,b\right)
be a pair in
S

that is minimal with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
,
that is:

\forall\,c,\,d.\;\left(c,\,d\right)\in{S}\implies c,d\in\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])\implies\left(c,\,d\right)\in\{\left(a,\,b\right),\left(b,\,a\right)\}.

Let
D
be the set
\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[\{a,b\}])
.

Claim 1

The events
a
and
b
belong to different threads.

Proof.

If either
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
or
\left(b,\,a\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
,
then
\left(a,\,b\right)
would not form a
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race.
∎

Claim 2

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[{\texttt{E}}\setminus\{a,b\}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]\subseteq{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*};\,[{\texttt{E}}\setminus\{a,b\}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]\setminus{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\neq\emptyset.

Then,
there must be an edge
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
such that

d\in\mathit{dom}([{\texttt{E}}\setminus\{a,b\}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]),

and
\left(c,\,d\right)\notin{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
.

We claim that
d\in\mathit{dom}(({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}});\,[\{a,b\}])
.
If
d\in\mathit{dom}(({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i});\,[\{a,b\}])
,
then the assertion follows immediately.
Moreover, if
d\in\mathit{dom}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e};\,[\{a,b\}])
, and if
d\notin\mathit{dom}(({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}});\,[\{a,b\}])
,
then it must be the case that
d\notin\mathit{dom}(({{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}});\,[\{a,b\}])
.
However, in this case,
either the pair
\left(d,\,a\right)
or the pair
\left(d,\,b\right)

would contradict the minimality of
\left(a,\,b\right)
.

We now proceed by induction on the number
of
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
steps between
c
and
d
.
In the base case, when
c=d
,
the desired conclusion follows from the previous paragraph.
In the inductive case, we have
e
such that
\left(c,\,e\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})

and
\left(e,\,d\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
.
We wish to prove that
\left(c,\,e\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
.
If not, then
the pair
\left(c,\,e\right)

forms a
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race.
The minimality of
\left(a,\,b\right)
implies that
\left(c,\,e\right)\in\{\left(a,\,b\right),\left(b,\,a\right)\}
.
However,
because
e\in\mathit{dom}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+};\,[\{a,b\}])
,
this contradicts either
No-Thin-Air
or the fact that
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{S}
.
∎

Claim 3

[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})};\,[\{a,b\}]=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The relation
R\triangleq[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})};\,[\{a,b\}]

is included in

\{\left(a,\,a\right),\left(a,\,b\right),\left(b,\,a\right),\left(b,\,b\right)\}.

The inclusions
\left(a,\,a\right)\in{R}
and
\left(b,\,b\right)\in{R}

contradict
No-Thin-Air
.
Therefore, if
R
is non-empty,
then it must be the case that either
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{R}

or
\left(b,\,a\right)\in{R}
.
Suppose by contradiction, and without loss of generality,
that
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{R}
.
Then, there exists
c
such that
\left(a,\,c\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}

and
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}
.
If
c\in\{a,b\}
,
then either
\left(a,\,c\right)

or
\left(c,\,b\right)

contradicts
No-Thin-Air
.
If
c\notin\{a,b\}
,
then, by
Claim 2
,
it follows that
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
,
a contradiction to the fact that
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{S}
.
∎

Claim 4

The events
a
and
b
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
(resp.
\left(b,\,a\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
),
then
b
(resp.
a
) is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})
-maximal.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{-1}
,
then
a
and
b
are
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})
-maximal.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction,
and without loss of generality,
that
a
is not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal,
and let
c
be an event in
D

such that
\left(a,\,c\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
.
Then it must be the case that
either
\left(c,\,a\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}

or
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
.
The first case contradicts
No-Thin-Air
.
If
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
then
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}
,
otherwise
c=b
and
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
,
a contradiction to
Claim 1
.
The inclusion
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}
,
however,
contradicts

Claim 3
,
because then
\left(a,\,b\right)\in[a];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[c];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,[b]\subseteq[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]
.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{-1}
,
then we prove, without loss of generality,
that
a
is
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})}
-maximal.
It suffices to show that
[a];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})}^{+}

is empty.
Suppose by contradiction that there exists
c\in D

such that
\left(a,\,c\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})

and
\left(c,\,\_\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})}^{*}
.
Because
a
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal,
the edge
\left(a,\,c\right)
belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D}
.
Moreover, because
c\in D

it is the case that either
\left(c,\,a\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}

or
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
.
The first case contradicts
No-Thin-Air
.
In the second case,
the edge
\left(c,\,b\right)
must also belong
to
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}
;
otherwise, the events
c
and
b
would coincide,
and therefore
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
,
a contradiction to the assumption
that
\left(a,\,b\right)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{-1}
.
The inclusion
\left(c,\,b\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}
,
however, contradicts

Claim 3
,
because then
\left(a,\,b\right)\in[a];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}};\,[c];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,[b]\subseteq[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]
.

Finally, we prove, without loss of generality,
that, if
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
,
then
b
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})
-maximal.
Because
b
is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal read event,
the relation
[b];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})

is empty.
Therefore, the relation
[b];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})}^{+}

is empty,
which conclusion finishes the proof.
∎

We now introduce the following sets:

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;\;}c@{\;\;}l@{}}D_{ab}&\triangleq&D\setminus\{a,b\}\\
D_{a}&\triangleq&D\setminus\{a\}\\
D_{b}&\triangleq&D\setminus\{b\}\end{array}

Claim 5

The set
D_{ab}
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
(resp.
\left(b,\,a\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
),
then
D_{b}
(resp.
D_{a}
) is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{-1}
,
then the sets
D_{a}
and
D_{b}
are both
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed.

Proof.

To show that
D_{ab}
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed,
it suffices to prove that the inclusion
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[D_{ab}]\subseteq[D_{ab}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
holds.
Suppose by contradiction that it does not,
and let
\left(c,\,d\right)
be a pair such that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[D_{ab}]

and

\left(c,\,d\right)\notin[D_{ab}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
.
Because
D
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed
(by definition),
and because
D_{ab}\subseteq D
,
it follows that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in[D];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
.
We thus conclude that
c\in\{a,b\}
,
because
\left(c,\,d\right)\in([D];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}))\setminus([D_{ab}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}))\subseteq[\{a,b\}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
.
Because
d\in D_{ab}\subseteq D
,
there exists
e\in\{a,b\}

such that
\left(d,\,e\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{*}
,
by definition of
D
.
In fact, it must be the case that
\left(d,\,e\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+}
,
because
d\notin\{a,b\}
.
We then reach a contradiction to

Claim 3
,
because
\left(c,\,e\right)\in[\{a,b\}];\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[d];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,[\{a,b\}];\,\subseteq[\{a,b\}];\,{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})}^{+};\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}});\,[\{a,b\}]
.
The remaining claims follow a similar proof.
∎

Claim 6

The graph
(G|_{D_{ab}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{ab}})

is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{ab}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that
{\texttt{race}}({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{ab}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})})\setminus({\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}\times{\texttt{E}}^{{\texttt{sc}}})

is non-empty,
and let
\left(c,\,d\right)
be a pair in this set.
We claim that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{S}
.
If not, then
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}\,\cup\,({({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+})^{-1}
.
Suppose without loss of generality
that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
.
Because
D_{ab}
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})
-closed,
and because
c,d\in D_{ab}
,
it follows that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{ab}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}^{+}
,
a contradiction with the fact that
\left(c,\,d\right)
forms a
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{ab}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race.
Therefore, it must be the case that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in{S}
.
Because
c,d\in D
,
it follows from the minimality of
\left(a,\,b\right)

that
\left(c,\,d\right)\in\{\left(a,\,b\right),\left(b,\,a\right)\}
,
a contradiction with the fact that
c,d\in D_{ab}
.
∎

Claim 7

The graph
(G|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{a}})

is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{a}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{a}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.

Analogously,
the graph
(G|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{b}})

is not
{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D_{b}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{b}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})}
-racy.

Proof.

Proof similar to
Claim 6
.
∎

Claim 8

The graph
(G|_{D_{ab}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{ab}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{ab}})

is
SC
-consistent.

Proof.

From
Claim 5

and Lemma
C.6
,
it follows that
G|_{D_{ab}}
is
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent.
Finally,
thanks to
Claim 6

and Lemma
C.11
,
it follows that
G|_{D_{ab}}
is
SC
-consistent.
∎

Claim 9

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
(resp.
\left(b,\,a\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
),
then
(G|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{b}})

(resp.
(G|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{a}})
)
is
SC
-consistent.

Proof.

Proof similar to
Claim 8
.
∎

Claim 10

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}^{-1}
,
then
(G|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{a}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{a}})

and
(G|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D_{b}},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D_{b}})

are
SC
-consistent.

Proof.

Proof similar to
Claim 8
.
∎

Claim 11

The graph
(G|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}|_{D})

is
SC
-consistent, and associated with
p
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
C.6
that
G|_{D}
is
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistent, and associated with
p
.
To prove that
G|_{D}
is
SC
-consistent,
suppose by contradiction that it is not,
and let
C
be a cycle that violates
SC-Coherence
.
Now, proceed by case disjunction on whether
\left(a,\,b\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})\,\cup\,({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})^{-1}
.
If it does not, then a cycle that violates
SC-Coherence
must be included in
D_{ab}
.
However, such a cycle would contradict

Claim 6
.
Let us consider the case in which
\left(a,\,b\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})\,\cup\,({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})^{-1}
,
and let us assume, without loss of generality,
that
\left(a,\,b\right)\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})
.
We now consider the following cases:

•

Case:
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
,
then, by
Claim 9
,
the graph
G|_{D_{b}}
is
SC
-consistent.
Therefore, the cycle
C
must include
b
,
otherwise it would violate the
SC
-consistency
of
G|_{D_{b}}
.
Because
b
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}
-maximal
(
Claim 4
),
the only edge that can follow
b
in
C

is a
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}
edge.
However, because
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}};\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
,
the sequence
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}};\,[b];\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}

can be replaced with a
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
edge that avoids
b
,
thereby yielding a cycle that violates
the
SC
-consistency of
G|_{D_{b}}
.

•

Case:
\left(a,\,b\right)\in({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})
.

If
\left(a,\,b\right)\in({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})
,
then, by
Claim 10
,
both
G|_{D_{a}}
and
G|_{D_{b}}
are
SC
-consistent.
Therefore, the cycle
C
must include
a
and
b
,
otherwise it would violate the
SC
-consistency
of either
G|_{D_{a}}
or
G|_{D_{b}}
.
Because
b
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{D}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{D})
-maximal
(
Claim 4
),
the only edge that can follow
b
in
C

is a
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
edge.
However, because
({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}});\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\subseteq({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})
,
the sequence
({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}});\,[b];\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}

can be replaced with a
({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})
edge that avoids
b
,
thereby yielding a cycle that violates
the
SC
-consistency of
G|_{D_{b}}
.

∎

The graph
G|_{D}
contradicts
the premise that
p
is
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race free,
because
the graph
G|_{D}
is
(1)
SC
-consistent;
(2) associated with
p
;
and
(3) contains a
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}|_{{\texttt{sc}}})
-race,
the pair
\left(a,\,b\right)
.
∎

C.2.
Extension Property

Theorem C.13 (
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
- Extension-I).

RC11
-consistency conditions are equivalent
to those of
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
in every execution graph
containing only
RC11
events.

Proof.

In the absence of
Ex86
events,
the relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}_{\textnormal{\scalebox{.70}{{RC11} }}}
is equivalent to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
,
and
the relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}
is equivalent to

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]\,\cup\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
.
Therefore,
the only differences between the two models,
with respect to the derived relations,
is the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
:
in
RC11
,
it is defined as
{({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{+}
,
whereas,
in
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
,
it is defined as
{({{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{+}
.
Let us use the names of these models as a prefix to
distinguish to which version of
{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}

(or of any other relation defined on top of
{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
)
we refer.

To complete the proof it is thus sufficient to show that

Coherence-II
and

Coherence-III
are a consequence of

RC11
-consistency, and that,
in the remaining
RC11
-consistency conditions,
the relations
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
and
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}

can be used interchangeably.
That is, it suffices to show that the following assertions hold:

(1)

\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})}\implies\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}

(2)

\mathit{acyclic}{(\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\sf{psc}})}\implies\mathit{acyclic}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})}

(3)

\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}}^{?}})}\iff\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}}^{?}})}

(4)

\mathit{acyclic}{(\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\sf{psc}})}\iff\mathit{acyclic}{(\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\sf{psc}})}

Proof of Assertion (1).

Immediate from

{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}

(which holds of
RC11
-events-only execution graphs).

Proof of Assertion (2).

We proceed by contradiction;
that is,
we show that,
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
is cyclic, then so is
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\sf{psc}}
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}c@{\;}l@{}}&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]\,\cup\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]\,\cup\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}(\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\;\;\textit{{\color[rgb]{0.9375,0.1602,0.1602}(Absurd)}
}\\
&\lor&\mathit{cyclic}([{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}])\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}([{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}])\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}([{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}])\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}(\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\sf{psc}})\end{array}\end{array}

Proof of Assertion (3).

Because
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}

is included in
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
,
the left-to-right implication is trivial.
The other direction follows by contradiction.
The proof exploits the equality
{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}});\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}^{?}

and the inclusion
[{\texttt{E}}\setminus{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{nt}}}];\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\;\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}}^{?})\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{\;\;}l@{}}\implies&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}(\overbrace{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}}^{?}}^{\subseteq\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}})\;\lor\;\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}}^{?})\\
\lor&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{?};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}}^{?})\;\lor\;\underbrace{\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{?};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}}^{?})}_{\implies\;\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}}^{?};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{?})}\\
\implies&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}}^{?})\;\lor\;\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})}^{?};\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}}^{?})\\
\implies&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}}^{?})\end{array}\end{array}

Proof of Assertion (4).

We show the following equality (which holds of
RC11
-events-only execution graphs):

[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]=[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]

Because
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}\subseteq\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
,
it is easy to see that the relation on the right-hand side of the
equality is included
in the relation on the left-hand side.
Let us now show the inclusion in the other direction:

[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]\subseteq[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,[{\texttt{F}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}]

Exploiting the equality
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}\,\cup\,({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}});\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}^{?}

and the inclusion
[{\texttt{E}}\setminus{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{nt}}}];\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
,
it is then easy to see that
every edge
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}
in
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}

can be merged into the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}}
edge
that either precedes or succeeds
\textsf{RC11}.{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}
.

∎

Theorem C.14 (
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
- Extension-II).

Ex86
-consistency conditions are equivalent
to those of
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
in every execution graph
containing only
Ex86
events.

Proof.

The proof is split into two parts.
First we prove that
Ex86
-consistency implies
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistency,
then we prove the converse:

(1)

Ex86
-consistency
\implies

\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistency.

It is thus sufficient to prove that following conditions hold:

•

Coherence-I
.

The proof follows by contradiction.
We prove that,
in the absence of
RC11
events,
the violation of
Coherence-I
leads
to the violation of
Ex86
-consistency
or to the violation of
Coherence-II
(which we show to hold in the next item):

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}l@{}}&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}}^{?})\\
&\implies\;\,\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}})\;\lor\;\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\\[8.53581pt]
&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}})\\
&\implies\;\,\mathit{cyclic}(\underbrace{[{\texttt{RMW}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}\cup{\texttt{R}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}}_{\subseteq\;\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e})\\
&\implies\;\,\mathit{cyclic}(\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e})\\
&\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\\[8.53581pt]
&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sf{hb}};\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\implies\;\,\neg\mathit{irreflexive}\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\overbrace{[\mathit{codom}({\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}_{\textnormal{\scalebox{.70}{{RC11} }}};\,{([{\texttt{F}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{sf}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}})}^{?};\,[{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}]}^{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\subseteq\;\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}[{\texttt{RMW}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}\cup{\texttt{R}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\;\cup\\
[{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{E}}\setminus{\texttt{R}}\setminus{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{nt}}}]\;\cup\\
[{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{nt}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,[{\texttt{RMW}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}\cup{\texttt{F}}^{\sqsupseteq{\texttt{sf}}}];\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}}^{?}\;\cup\\
[{\texttt{W}}^{{\texttt{nt}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{\textnormal{{loc}}}\end{array}\right)\end{array}}\\
{({{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e};\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}}^{?})}^{+};\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}}\end{array}\end{array}\right)\\
&\implies\;\,\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}};\,{({{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e};\,{(\underbrace{[{\texttt{R}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}\cup{\texttt{RMW}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}}_{\subseteq\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}})}^{?})}^{+};\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\implies\;\,\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\end{array}

•

Coherence-II
.

It suffices to exploit the inclusion

({{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{i}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{i})\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{\textnormal{{loc}}};\,[{\texttt{E}}\setminus{\texttt{R}}]\subseteq\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}

to show that the violation of
Coherence-II
leads
to the violation of
External
:

\begin{array}[]{@{~~~~~~~~}r@{\;}c@{\;}l@{}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \lx@intercol\hfil\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}((\underbrace{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{i}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{i}}_{\subseteq\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}})\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}(\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e})\end{array}

•

Coherence-III
. (Immediate from
Internal
.)

•

No-Thin-Air
.

We show that the negation of

No-Thin-Air
leads to a contradiction with
External
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}c@{\;}l@{}}\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}})&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}((\underbrace{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i}}_{\subseteq\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}})\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}((\underbrace{[{\texttt{R}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}\,\cup\,{\texttt{RMW}}^{{\texttt{tso}}}];\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}}_{\subseteq\,\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}})\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}(\textsf{Ex86}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{e}\,\cup\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{e})\end{array}

(2)

\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-consistency
\implies

Ex86
-consistency.

Condition
External
is an immediate consequence of
Coherence-II
.
The proof of Condition
Internal
is split into the three following
subconditions:

•

\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}}_{i})}
. (Immediate from
No-Thin-Air
.)

•

\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{i})}
.

The violation of this condition leads to a contradiction with

Coherence-II
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\;}c@{\;\,}l@{}}\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{i})&\implies&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}((\underbrace{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}|_{\textnormal{{loc}}};\,[{\texttt{E}}\setminus{\texttt{R}}]}_{\subseteq\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}});\,{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}}_{i})\\
&\implies&\neg\mathit{irreflexive}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}};\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\\
&\implies&\mathit{cyclic}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{ppo}}_{\textnormal{{asm}}}\,\cup\,{\color[rgb]{0.9,0,0}\sf{eco}})\end{array}

•

\mathit{irreflexive}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}};\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\sf{rb}}}_{i})}
. (Immediate from
Coherence-III
.)

∎

Appendix D
Compilation

D.1.
Compilation Schemes

We present two compilation schemes
from
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-lang to
Ex86
-lang:
one that naturally extends the scheme studied by
Lahav et al. (2017, Fig. 8)
,
and a slightly more elaborated one that
maps
rlx
writes to non-temporal stores,
and adds store fences
to the mapping of
rel
/
sc
writes and
to the mapping of
rel
/
acqrel
fences.
We prove that these schemes are correct (with respect to
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
)
in
§

D.3
.

Definition D.1 (Compilation Scheme from
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
-lang to
Ex86
-lang).

\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{~~}c@{~~}c@{}}\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{\;}l@{}}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}{\texttt{;}}\,{\texttt{mfence}}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{\neq{\texttt{sc}}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0ptr{\texttt{:=}}\,{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{\mathit{md}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&r{\texttt{:=}}\,{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}\end{array}\hfil\penalty\ \penalty\ &\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{\;}l@{}}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{fence}}_{{\texttt{sc}}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{mfence}}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{fence}}_{\neq{\texttt{sc}}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{skip}}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{if}}\penalty\ e\penalty\ {\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}}\penalty\ s\penalty\ {\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{if}}\penalty\ e\penalty\ {\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}}\penalty\ \llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pts\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\penalty\ {\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}}\end{array}\hfil\penalty\ \penalty\ &\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{\;}l@{}}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pts{\texttt{;}}\,s^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pts\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis{\texttt{;}}\,\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pts^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{skip}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{skip}}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{{asm}}}\,{\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}}s{\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis&\;\triangleq&s\end{array}\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{~~~~}c@{}}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0ptr\,{\texttt{:=}}\,{\texttt{rmw}}_{\mathit{md}}\,({\texttt{[}}e_{1}{\texttt{]}},\,e_{2},\,e_{3})\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\;\triangleq\;r\,{\texttt{:=}}\,{\texttt{rmw}}\,({\texttt{[}}e_{1}{\texttt{]}},\,e_{2},\,e_{3})\hfil\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ &\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{while}}\;e\;{\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}}\,s\,{\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\;\triangleq\;{\texttt{while}}\;e\;{\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}}\,\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pts\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\,{\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}}\end{array}\end{array}

Definition D.2 (Alternative Compilation Scheme).

Same as Def.
D.1
except for the following cases:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\begin{array}[]{r@{}c@{\;}l}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{{\texttt{sc}}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\textnormal{-alt}&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{sfence}}{\texttt{;}}\,{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}{\texttt{;}}\,{\texttt{mfence}}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{{\texttt{rel}}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\textnormal{-alt}&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{sfence}}{\texttt{;}}\,{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{r@{}c@{\;}l}\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{[}}e{\texttt{]}}^{{\texttt{rlx}}}{\texttt{:=}}\,e^{\prime}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\textnormal{-alt}&\;\triangleq&[e]{\texttt{:=}}_{{\texttt{nt}}}\,e^{\prime}\\
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt{\texttt{fence}}_{{\texttt{rel}},\,{\texttt{acqrel}}}\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis\textnormal{-alt}&\;\triangleq&{\texttt{sfence}}\end{array}\end{array}

D.2.
Mixed Execution Graphs

Our proofs of compilation correctness
(
§

D.3
)
rely on the novel notion of
mixed execution graphs
,
a type of execution graph whose nodes
contain events from both the source-level and target-level models.
Before presenting our compilation-correctness results,
let us give a brief introduction to mixed execution graphs.

Informally speaking,
a mixed execution graph is the
superposition of two execution graphs:
one called
source graph
,
which is associated with a source program
p
;
and
one called
target graph
,
which is associated with the compilation of
p
.
The key feature of a mixed execution graph is that
it captures the fact that source and target graphs
share the same overall structure.
Indeed,
because a compilation scheme preserves the
control flow of the source program and
changes only how memory operations are mapped
to operations in the target language,
for every execution graph of the compiled program,
one can always construct an execution graph of the
source program that preserves much of the structure
of the target graph,
including its primitive relations
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sf{po}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\sf{rf}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\sf{mo}}
.
The only mismatches between these graphs come from how
one memory operation from the source language might be
mapped to
zero, one, or multiple memory operations from the target language.

To account for these mismatches,
nodes in a mixed graph,
called
mixed nodes
,
carry events from both source and target models.
Events from the two models however cannot be
arbitrarily assembled in a mixed node:
the source-level events in a mixed node correspond
to the events of a single source instruction
and the target-level events correspond to the
events emitted by the snippet of target-level language
produced by the mapping of this instruction.
Therefore,
the range of mixed nodes is fixed and
determined by the underlying compilation scheme.

Mixed graphs form a very convenient tool for proving
compilation-correctness results
because they allow
one to work with the execution graphs from both the source
program and its compiled version at the same time, and
because they allow one to forget about the compilation
scheme which is ultimately encoded in the set of permissible
mixed nodes.
Moreover, it is possible to lift the consistency conditions from
the models of source and target languages to this mixed-graph structure.
Both models can thus be defined on the same structure,
thereby allowing one to formally reason about statements of the kind
“
one model is stronger than the other
”.
In fact, the main convenience of mixed execution graphs is
precisely to allow one to formulate the compilation correctness result
as a statement in this fashion:
“
in a mixed execution graph with nodes taken from a well-chosen set,
if the consistency conditions of the target model hold,
then so do the consistency conditions of the source model
”.
The set of nodes has to be well chosen so as to correctly reflect
the compilation scheme begin considered.

To give an illustration of mixed execution graphs,
let us consider
\textsf{RC11}^{\textsf{Ex86}}
as the source model,

Ex86
as the target model,
and
\llparenthesis\hskip 2.0pt\_\hskip 1.0pt\rrparenthesis
(Definition
D.1
)
as the compilation scheme.
